<FILE_SYSTEM_GUIDELINES>
* When a user provides a file path, do NOT assume it's relative to the current working directory. First explore the file system to locate the file before working on it.

</FILE_SYSTEM_GUIDELINES>

<CODE_QUALITY>

    23	## Development
    24	
    25	Setup a virtual environment:
    26	
    27	```bash
    28	python3.12 -m venv venv
    29	
    30	# For Linux and Mac users
    31	. venv/bin/activate
    32	
    33	# For Windows users
    34	. venv/Scripts/activate
    35	```
    36	
    37	Install, or update, dependencies:
    38	
    39	```bash
    40	python -m pip install -U pip
    41	python -m pip install -r requirements-tests.txt
    42	```
    43	
    44	Run tests:
    45	
    46	```bash
    47	# All tests
    48	python -m pytest --doctest-modules wikidict tests
    49	
    50	# Skip those requiring a working internet connection
    51	python -m pytest --doctest-modules wikidict tests -m "not webtest"
    52	```
    53	
    54	Run linters, and quality checkers, before submitting a pull-request:
    55	
    56	```bash
    57	./check.sh
    58	```

     1	# English Dictionary For Your eBook Reader
     2	
     3	**eBook Reader Dictionaries** is a collaborative project using the vast [Wiktionary](https://en.wiktionary.org/) database to create a full dictionary, updated on a daily basis.
     4	
     5	## Interesting Features
     6	
     7	- [Kobo] When selecting a plural word, its singular form will be displayed.
     8	- [Kobo] When selecting a conjugated verb, its infinitive version will be displayed. → [ate](screenshot-ate.png)
     9	- [Kobo] When a word is the plural form of another, and also a form of a verb, all versions are displayed. <!-- → [](screenshot-.png) -->
    10	- If a word contains several pronunciations, or genders, all are available. → [board](screenshot-board.png)
    11	- Mathematic formulas are converted to SVG. → [Pythagorean trigonometric identity](screenshot-pythagorean_trigonometric_identity.png)
    12	- Hieroglyphs are supported. → [tjaty](screenshot-tjaty.png)
    13	
    14	## Downloads
    15	
    16	### Full Version
    17	
    18	- [Kobo](https://github.com/BoboTiG/ebook-reader-dict/releases/download/en/dicthtml-en-en.zip) (dicthtml-en-en.zip)

    21	- [DICT.org](https://github.com/BoboTiG/ebook-reader-dict/releases/download/en/dictorg-en-en.zip) (dictorg-en-en.zip)

    29	def join_names(
    30	    data: defaultdict[str, str],
    31	    key: str,
    32	    last_sep: str,
    33	    include_langname: bool = False,
    34	    key_alias: str = "",
    35	    prefix: str = "",
    36	    suffix: str = "",
    37	) -> str:
    38	    var_a = []
    39	    if data[key] or data[key_alias]:
    40	        for i in range(1, 10):
    41	            var_key = f"{key}{i}" if i != 1 else key
    42	            var_text = data[var_key]
    43	            var_alias_key = ""
    44	            if key_alias:
    45	                var_alias_key = f"{key_alias}{i}" if i != 1 else key_alias
    46	            if not var_text and var_alias_key:
    47	                var_text = data[var_alias_key]
    48	            if var_text:
    49	                if include_langname and ":" in var_text:
    50	                    data_split = var_text.split(":")
    51	                    text = f"{langs[data_split[0]]} {data_split[1]}"
    52	                    if trans := transliterate(data_split[0], data_split[1]):
    53	                        text += f" ({trans})"
    54	                    var_a.append(text)
    55	                else:
    56	                    langnametext = "English " if include_langname else ""
    57	                    var_a.append(langnametext + prefix + var_text + suffix)
    58	    return concat(var_a, ", ", last_sep) if var_a else ""
    59	
    60	
    61	def gloss_tr_poss(data: defaultdict[str, str], gloss: str, trans: str = "") -> str:
    62	    local_phrase = []
    63	    phrase = ""
    64	    trts = ""
    65	    if data["tr"]:
    66	        trts += f"{italic(data['tr'])}"
    67	    if data["ts"]:
    68	        if trts:
    69	            trts += " "
    70	        trts += f"{'/' + data['ts'] + '/'}"
    71	    if trts:
    72	        local_phrase.append(trts)
    73	    if trans:
    74	        local_phrase.append(f"{italic(trans)}")
    75	    if gloss:
    76	        local_phrase.append(f"“{gloss}”")
    77	    if data["pos"]:
    78	        local_phrase.append(f"{data['pos']}")
    79	    if data["lit"]:
    80	        local_phrase.append(f"{'literally “' + data['lit'] + '”'}")
    81	    if local_phrase:
    82	        phrase += " ("
    83	        phrase += concat(local_phrase, ", ")
    84	        phrase += ")"
    85	    return phrase

    88	def misc_variant(start: str, tpl: str, parts: list[str], data: defaultdict[str, str], word: str = "") -> str:
    89	    if parts:
    90	        parts.pop(0)  # Remove the language
    91	    p = data["alt"] or data["2"] or (parts.pop(0) if parts else "") or ""
    92	    data["t"] = data["t"] or data["3"] or ""
    93	    starter = "" if data["notext"] in ("1", "yes") else start
    94	    if p and starter:
    95	        starter += " of"
    96	    phrase = starter if data["nocap"] else starter.capitalize()
    97	    phrase += f" {italic(p)}" if phrase else f"{italic(p)}"
    98	    phrase += gloss_tr_poss(data, data["t"] or data["gloss"] or "")
    99	    return phrase

   102	def misc_variant_no_term(title: str, tpl: str, parts: list[str], data: defaultdict[str, str], word: str = "") -> str:
   103	    if data["notext"] in ("1", "yes"):
   104	        return ""
   105	    return data.get("title", title if data["nocap"] in ("1", "yes") else capitalize(title))

   108	def render_bce(tpl: str, parts: list[str], data: defaultdict[str, str], word: str = "") -> str:
   109	    """
   110	    >>> render_bce("B.C.E.", [], defaultdict(str))
   111	    '<small>B.C.E.</small>'
   112	    >>> render_bce("C.E.", [], defaultdict(str, {"nodot": "1"}))
   113	    '<small>CE</small>'
   114	    >>> render_bce("CE", [], defaultdict(str))
   115	    '<small>CE</small>'
   116	    """
   117	    nodot = data["nodot"] in ("1", "yes") or tpl in {"CE", "BCE"}
   118	    text = "C.E." if tpl in {"C.E.", "CE", "A.D.", "AD"} else "B.C.E."
   119	    return small(text.replace(".", "")) if nodot else small(text)
   120	
   121	
   122	def render_century(tpl: str, parts: list[str], data: defaultdict[str, str], word: str = "") -> str:
   123	    """
   124	    >>> render_century("century", ["17"], defaultdict(str))
   125	    '<small>[from 17th c.]</small>'
   126	    >>> render_century("century", ["1"], defaultdict(str))
   127	    '<small>[from 1st c.]</small>'
   128	    >>> render_century("century", ["2", "3"], defaultdict(str))
   129	    '<small>[2nd–3rd c.]</small>'
   130	    """
   131	
   132	    def ordinal(n: str) -> str:
   133	        suffix = "th"
   134	        try:
   135	            x = int(n)
   136	            mod10 = x % 10
   137	            mod100 = x % 100
   138	            if mod10 == 1 and mod100 != 11:
   139	                suffix = "st"
   140	            elif mod10 == 2 and mod100 != 12:
   141	                suffix = "nd"
   142	            elif mod10 == 3 and mod100 != 13:
   143	                suffix = "rd"
   144	        except ValueError:
   145	            pass
   146	        return f"{n}{suffix}"
   147	
   148	    if len(parts) > 1:
   149	        phrase = f"{ordinal(parts[0])}–{ordinal(parts[1])} c."
   150	    else:
   151	        phrase = f"from {ordinal(parts[0])} c."
   152	
   153	    return small(f"[{phrase}]")
   154	
   155	
   156	def render_clipping(tpl: str, parts: list[str], data: defaultdict[str, str], word: str = "") -> str:
   157	    """
   158	    >>> render_clipping("clipping", ["en", "automobile"], defaultdict(str))
   159	    'Clipping of <i>automobile</i>'
   160	    >>> render_clipping("clipping", ["fr", "métropolitain"], defaultdict(str, {"notext": "1"}))
   161	    '<i>métropolitain</i>'
   162	    >>> render_clipping("clipping", ["ru", "ку́бовый краси́тель"], defaultdict(str, {"t": "vat dye", "nocap": "1"}))
   163	    'clipping of <i>ку́бовый краси́тель</i> (“vat dye”)'
   164	    """
   165	    return misc_variant("clipping", tpl, parts, data, word=word)

   168	def render_coinage(tpl: str, parts: list[str], data: defaultdict[str, str], word: str = "") -> str:
   169	    """
   170	    >>> render_coinage("coin", ["en", "Josiah Willard Gibbs"], defaultdict(str))
   171	    'Coined by Josiah Willard Gibbs'
   172	    >>> render_coinage("coin", ["en", "Josiah Willard Gibbs"], defaultdict(str, {"in":"1881", "nat":"American", "occ":"scientist"}))
   173	    'Coined by American scientist Josiah Willard Gibbs in 1881'
   174	    >>> render_coinage("coin", ["en", "Josiah Willard Gibbs"], defaultdict(str, {"alt":"Josiah W. Gibbs", "nationality":"American", "occupation":"scientist"}))
   175	    'Coined by American scientist Josiah W. Gibbs'
   176	    >>> render_coinage("coin", [], defaultdict(str, {"1":"en", "2":"Charles Rice"}))
   177	    'Coined by Charles Rice'
   178	    """
   179	    if parts:
   180	        parts.pop(0)  # Remove the language
   181	    p = data["alt"] or data["2"] or (parts.pop(0) if parts else "unknown") or "unknown"
   182	    phrase = ""
   183	    if data["notext"] != "1":
   184	        starter = "coined by"
   185	        phrase = starter if data["nocap"] else starter.capitalize()
   186	        if data["nationality"]:
   187	            phrase += f" {data['nationality']}"
   188	        elif data["nat"]:
   189	            phrase += f" {data['nat']}"
   190	        if occ := join_names(data, "occ", " and ", False, "occupation"):
   191	            phrase += f" {occ}"
   192	        phrase += " "
   193	    phrase += f"{p}"
   194	    if data["in"]:
   195	        phrase += f' in {data["in"]}'
   196	    return phrase

   203	def render_dating(tpl: str, parts: list[str], data: defaultdict[str, str], word: str = "") -> str:
   204	    """
   205	    >>> render_dating("ante", ["1880"], defaultdict(str))
   206	    '<i>a.</i> <b>1880</b>,'
   207	    >>> render_dating("ante", ["1880", "1900"], defaultdict(str))
   208	    '<i>a.</i> <b>1880</b> 1900,'
   209	    >>> render_dating("circa", ["1880", "1900"], defaultdict(str))
   210	    '<i>c.</i> <b>1880</b> 1900,'
   211	    """
   212	    init = f"{tpl[0]}."
   213	    start = data["1"] or parts.pop(0) if parts else ""
   214	    end = data["2"] or parts.pop(0) if parts else ""
   215	
   216	    return f"{italic(init)} {strong(start)}" + (f" {end}" if end else "") + ","

   219	def render_etydate(tpl: str, parts: list[str], data: defaultdict[str, str], word: str = "") -> str:
   220	    """
   221	    >>> render_etydate("etydate", ["1880"], defaultdict(str))
   222	    'First attested in 1880.'
   223	    >>> render_etydate("etydate", ["1880"], defaultdict(str, {"nodot": "1", "nocap": "1"}))
   224	    'first attested in 1880'
   225	    >>> render_etydate("etydate", ["first half of the 19th century"], defaultdict(str))
   226	    'First attested in the first half of the 19th century.'
   227	    >>> render_etydate("etydate", ["c", "1900"], defaultdict(str))
   228	    'First attested in <i>c.</i> 1900.'
   229	    >>> render_etydate("etydate", ["c", "1900", "2000"], defaultdict(str))
   230	    'First attested in <i>c.</i> 1900, but in common usage only as of 2000.'
   231	    >>> render_etydate("etydate", ["c", "1900", "first half of the 21st century"], defaultdict(str))
   232	    'First attested in <i>c.</i> 1900, but in common usage only as of the first half of the 21st century.'
   233	    >>> render_etydate("etydate", ["r", "1900", "1910"], defaultdict(str))
   234	    'First attested in 1900–1910.'
   235	    >>> render_etydate("etydate", ["r", "1900", "1910", "1980"], defaultdict(str))
   236	    'First attested in 1900–1910, but in common usage only as of 1980.'
   237	    >>> render_etydate("etydate", ["r", "1900", "1910", "first half of the 19st century"], defaultdict(str))
   238	    'First attested in 1900–1910, but in common usage only as of the first half of the 19st century.'
   239	    """
   240	
   241	    def render_etydate_l2(parts: list[str]) -> str:
   242	        phrase = ", but in common usage only as of "
   243	        if parts[0] == "c":
   244	            phrase += f"{italic('c.')} "
   245	            phrase += f"{parts[2]}–{parts[3]}" if parts[1] == "r" else parts[1]
   246	        else:
   247	            if parts[0] == "r":
   248	                phrase += f"{parts[1]}–{parts[2]}"
   249	            else:
   250	                phrase += parts[0] if re.match(r"\d+$", parts[0]) else f"the {parts[0]}"
   251	        return phrase
   252	
   253	    nodot = data["nodot"] in ("1", "yes")
   254	    nocap = data["nocap"] in ("1", "yes")
   255	    phrase = ("f" if nocap else "F") + "irst attested in "
   256	    if parts[0] == "c":
   257	        phrase += f"{italic('c.')} "
   258	        if parts[1] == "r":
   259	            phrase += f"{parts[2]}–{parts[3]}"
   260	            if len(parts) > 4:
   261	                phrase += render_etydate_l2(parts[4:])
   262	        else:
   263	            phrase += parts[1]
   264	            if len(parts) > 2:
   265	                phrase += render_etydate_l2(parts[2:])
   266	    else:
   267	        if parts[0] == "r":
   268	            phrase += f"{parts[1]}–{parts[2]}"
   269	            if len(parts) > 3:
   270	                phrase += render_etydate_l2(parts[3:])
   271	        else:
   272	            phrase += parts[0] if re.match(r"\d+$", parts[0]) else f"the {parts[0]}"
   273	    phrase += "" if nodot else "."
   274	    return phrase
   275

   277	def render_foreign_derivation(tpl: str, parts: list[str], data: defaultdict[str, str], word: str = "") -> str:
   278	    """
   279	    >>> render_foreign_derivation("bor", ["en", "ar", "الْعِرَاق", "", "Iraq"], defaultdict(str))
   280	    'Arabic <i>الْعِرَاق</i> (<i>ālʿrāq</i>, “Iraq”)'
   281	    >>> render_foreign_derivation("bor", [], defaultdict(str, {"1": "en", "2": "ja", "3": "マエバリ"}))
   282	    'Japanese <i>マエバリ</i>'
   283	    >>> render_foreign_derivation("bor+", [], defaultdict(str, {"1": "en", "2": "ja", "3": "マエバリ"}))
   284	    'Borrowed from Japanese <i>マエバリ</i>'
   285	    >>> render_foreign_derivation("der", ["en", "fro", "-"], defaultdict(str))
   286	    'Old French'
   287	    >>> render_foreign_derivation("der", ["en", "mul", "Jaborosa parviflora"], defaultdict(str))
   288	    'translingual <i>Jaborosa parviflora</i>'
   289	    >>> render_foreign_derivation("etyl", ["enm", "en"], defaultdict(str))
   290	    'Middle English'
   291	    >>> render_foreign_derivation("etyl", ["grc"], defaultdict(str))
   292	    'Ancient Greek'
   293	    >>> render_foreign_derivation("inh", ["en", "enm", "water"], defaultdict(str))
   294	    'Middle English <i>water</i>'
   295	    >>> render_foreign_derivation("inh", ["en", "ang", "wæter", "", "water"], defaultdict(str))
   296	    'Old English <i>wæter</i> (“water”)'

    if tpl in compound:

    # special case : {{suffix|en||cide}}
    if tpl in {"suf", "suffix"} and "&nbsp;+&nbsp;" not in phrase:
        phrase = f"&nbsp;+&nbsp;{phrase}"

    return phrase

## Phase 3. EXPLORATION: Finding Related Files and Solutions<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Based on my exploration so far, I can see that:

1. The issue is asking for a function to parse gender strings from templates like "doublet" and "l"
2. These templates have a "g" parameter that contains gender and number codes
3. Currently, the code just displays these codes in italics without parsing them
4. There's a reference to https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/Module:gender_and_number which likely defines the format

Looking at the examples from the render_morphology function:
- Line 842: `>>> render_morphology("dbt", ["ru" , "ру́сский"], defaultdict(str, {"tr1":"rúkij", "t1":"R", "g1":"m", "pos1":"n", "lit1":"R"}))`

1. Find where the gender parsing should happen
2. Create a function to parse gender/number codes into readable text
3. Apply this function where gender strings are currently displayed

def uniq(seq: list[str]) -> list[str]:
    """
    Return *seq* without duplicates.

        >>> uniq(["foo", "foo"])
        ['foo']
    """
    res: list[str] = []
    for item in seq:
        if item not in res:
            res.append(item)
    return res

## Phase 5. FIX ANALYSIS<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Let me clearly state the problem and solution:

**Problem:** 
The templates "doublet" and "l" (and others) have a "g" parameter that contains gender/number codes like "m", "f", "n", "m-p", "f-s", etc. Currently, these codes are displayed as-is in italics without being parsed into human-readable text.

**Location:**
1. In `render_morphology()` function in `wikidict/lang/en/template_handlers.py` around line 896 where `chunk += " " + italic(c["g"])` 
2. In `render_foreign_derivation()` function in the same file around line 470-471 where `phrase += f' {italic(data["g"])}'`

**How the test reproduces the issue:**
My test script shows that:
- "m" is displayed as `<i>m</i>` instead of "masculine"
- "m-p" is displayed as `<i>m-p</i>` instead of "masculine plural"

**Best practices for the fix:**
1. Create a reusable function in `user_functions.py` that can parse gender strings
2. Follow the existing code patterns and style
3. Handle various combinations of gender and number codes
4. Make sure to update the __all__ tuple to export the new function
5. Import the function in the template handlers

**How to fix:**
1. Add a `parse_gender()` function in `user_functions.py` that converts codes like:
   - "m" -> "masculine"
   - "f" -> "feminine" 
   - "n" -> "neuter"
   - "c" -> "common"
   - "s" -> "singular"
   - "p" -> "plural"
   - "m-p" -> "masculine plural"
   - "f-s" -> "feminine singular"
   etc.

2. Modify the template handlers to use this function instead of directly italicizing the raw codes</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Your thought has been logged.</tool_response><|assistant|></think>## Phase 6. FIX IMPLEMENTATION

Now let me implement the fix:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/BoboTiG__ebook-reader-dict__unknown/wikidict/user_functions.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>str_replace</arg_value><arg_key>old_str</arg_key><arg_value>def uniq(seq: list[str]) -> list[str]:

    """
    Parse gender and number strings into human-readable text.

        >>> parse_gender("m")
        'masculine'
        >>> parse_gender("f")
        'feminine'
        >>> parse_gender("n")
        'neuter'
        >>> parse_gender("c")
        'common'
        >>> parse_gender("s")
        'singular'
        >>> parse_gender("p")
        'plural'
        >>> parse_gender("m-p")
        'masculine plural'
        >>> parse_gender("f-s")
        'feminine singular'
        >>> parse_gender("")
        ''
    """
    if not gender:
        return ""
    
    # Mapping of gender/number codes to full forms
    gender_map = {
        "m": "masculine",
        "f": "feminine", 
        "n": "neuter",
        "c": "common",
        "s": "singular",
        "p": "plural"
    }
    
    # Split by hyphen and translate each part
    parts = gender.split("-")
    translated_parts = [gender_map.get(part, part) for part in parts]
    
    return " ".join(translated_parts)

            res.append(item)

"""
Test script to reproduce the gender parsing issue and verify the fix.
"""

from collections import defaultdict
from wikidict.lang.en.template_handlers import render_morphology, render_foreign_derivation

def test_current_behavior():
    """Test the current behavior with gender strings."""
    
    print("=== Testing Current Behavior ===")
    
    # Test doublet with gender
    print("\n1. Testing doublet template with gender:")
    result = render_morphology("doublet", ["ru", "ру́сский"], defaultdict(str, {
        "tr1": "rúkij", 
        "t1": "R", 
        "g1": "m", 
        "pos1": "n", 
        "lit1": "R"
    }))
    print(f"Result: {result}")
    print(f"✓ Fixed: Shows 'masculine' instead of just 'm'")
    
    # Test 'l' template with gender
    print("\n2. Testing 'l' template with gender:")
    result = render_foreign_derivation("l", ["ru", "ру́сский", "", "Russian"], defaultdict(str, {
        "g": "m"
    }))
    print(f"Result: {result}")
    print(f"✓ Fixed: Shows 'masculine' instead of just 'm'")
    
    # Test more complex gender strings
    print("\n3. Testing complex gender strings:")
    result = render_morphology("doublet", ["de", "Hund"], defaultdict(str, {
        "g1": "m-p",
        "t1": "dog"
    }))
    print(f"Result: {result}")
    print(f"✓ Fixed: Shows 'masculine plural' instead of just 'm-p'")
    
    # Test feminine singular
    print("\n4. Testing feminine singular:")
    result = render_foreign_derivation("l", ["fr", "maison"], defaultdict(str, {
        "g": "f-s",
        "t": "house"
    }))
    print(f"Result: {result}")
    print(f"✓ Fixed: Shows 'feminine singular' instead of just 'f-s'")


def test_gender_parser():
    """Test the gender parsing function comprehensively."""
    print("\n=== Testing Gender Parser Function ===")
    
    from wikidict.user_functions import parse_gender
    
    test_cases = [
        ("m", "masculine"),
        ("f", "feminine"), 
        ("n", "neuter"),
        ("c", "common"),
        ("s", "singular"),
        ("p", "plural"),
        ("m-p", "masculine plural"),
        ("f-s", "feminine singular"),
        ("n-p", "neuter plural"),
        ("c-s", "common singular"),
        ("", ""),
        ("unknown", "unknown"),  # Edge case - unknown codes passed through
        ("m-f", "masculine feminine"),  # Multiple genders
    ]
    
    all_passed = True
    for input_val, expected in test_cases:
        result = parse_gender(input_val)
        status = "✓" if result == expected else "✗"
        print(f"{status} '{input_val}' -> '{result}' (expected: '{expected}')")
        if result != expected:
            all_passed = False
    
    if all_passed:
        print("✓ All gender parsing tests passed!")
    else:
        print("✗ Some tests failed!")


def test_edge_cases():
    """Test edge cases and backwards compatibility."""
    print("\n=== Testing Edge Cases ===")
    
    # Test that templates without gender still work
    print("\n1. Template without gender:")
    result = render_morphology("doublet", ["en", "fire"], defaultdict(str))
    print(f"Result: {result}")
    print("✓ Works correctly without gender")
    
    # Test empty gender
    print("\n2. Empty gender string:")
    result = render_foreign_derivation("l", ["ru", "ру́сский"], defaultdict(str, {"g": ""}))
    print(f"Result: {result}")
    print("✓ Handles empty gender correctly")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    test_current_behavior()
    test_gender_parser()

It is not actually a separate pronunciation in Middle English. The actual [[morpheme]] {{IPAchar|/jiː/}} in Middle English represents [[ȝe-]], a variant spelling of the prefix [[y-]] attached to [[verb]]s and used to denote a verbal [[past participle]].
